Built: 2026-10-27

Cacti (tm) Documentation


[Image: Cacti] <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/images/logo.svg>


Cacti collects measurements from network devices, servers, and applications
and uses RRDtool to store time-series data and display graphs. Use this manual
to install Cacti, configure monitoring, and keep your installation running.


Version: This manual follows develop, the unreleased Cacti 1.3 series.
For released Cacti 1.2 installations, use the 1.2.x manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/tree/1.2.x> and
the requirements supplied with your installed version.


[Image: Example Cacti graph with populated historical data] <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/images/graphs-edit.png>


This example from Graph Overview <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Graph-Overview.md> contains historical sample
data and shows an earlier interface. Menu labels can differ in your version.


Current editions and contributed archives <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Other-Manuals.md> include PDF,
text, localized installation guides, and the development schema reference.


What Do You Want to Do


* Install or upgrade Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#install-or-upgrade-cacti>:
Check requirements, choose your platform, install, upgrade, and secure Cacti.


* Create your first graphs <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#create-your-first-graphs>:
Add a device, configure SNMP, create graphs, and verify data collection.


* Manage your installation <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#manage-your-installation>:
Organize devices and graphs, automate discovery, manage access, and report.


* Configure data collection <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#configure-data-collection>:
Set up collectors, Spine, remote polling, scheduling, and performance.


* Create and reuse templates <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#create-and-reuse-templates>:
Work with templates, data queries, and package import and export.


* Troubleshoot a problem <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/README.md#troubleshoot-a-problem>:
Investigate missing data, failed SNMP queries, and polling errors.




Getting Started


Follow this sequence for a new installation:


* Check requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md>.

* Install Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/General-Installing-Instructions.md> for your platform.

* Add a device and create its graphs <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/How-to-Graph-Your-Network.md>.

* Verify data collection <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Data-Debug.md> and check the
Cacti log <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Cacti-Log.md>.

* Secure the installation <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Hardening-Cacti-Installation.md> before making
it available to other users.



For the concepts behind these steps, read
Principles of Operation <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Principles-of-Operation.md>. To find your way around
Cacti, use Navigating the User Interface <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Navigating-The-User-Interface.md>.


Troubleshoot a Problem


* Graphs have no data <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Data-Debug.md>: Check polling and RRD updates.


* Find errors in the log <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Cacti-Log.md>: Inspect collection and system
logs.


* Investigate SNMP and graph failures <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Debugging.md>: Follow the debugging
guide for polling and rendering problems.


* Common questions and polling problems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Frequently-Asked-Questions.md>:
Find answers about SNMP, scheduling, password recovery, and graph errors.


* Known issues <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Known-Issues.md>: Check documented limitations and where
to report a problem.




Manual and Reference


Browse the complete manual index <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md> for every guide,
including procedures grouped with their related subjects.


Use the settings reference <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#settings-reference>,
command-line scripts <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Command-Line-Scripts.md>, and
graph and data reference <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#graph-and-data-reference> when you
need a particular option, command, variable, or graph expression. Check
release notes <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Version-Specific-Release-Notes.md> for version-specific changes.


Extend and Contribute


* Use and develop plugins <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#plugins>: Install extensions or
find plugin guidelines, hooks, and references.


* Contribute to Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Contributing.md>: Report bugs, improve code or
documentation, and help translate Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Contributing-Translations.md>.


* HTML manual content and publication <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resources/build/HTML-Manual.md>:
Build all chapters and preserve links from the archived HTML manual.


* Development standards <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Manual-Index.md#development-standards>: Follow
the documentation, PHP, SQL, and security conventions for your target branch.


* Architecture decisions <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Architecture-Decisions.md>: Read proposals and
accepted decisions guiding development.


* Video tutorials <https://www.youtube.com/channel/UC6ROmUGuuLa9BdHWzvGSY1w>: Watch tutorials on the official Cacti channel.
Check the demonstrated version against your installation.




Project Information


See project credits, acknowledgments, and licensing <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Project-Information.md>.
For downloads and news, visit the Cacti website <https://www.cacti.net/>.
For community support, use the Cacti Forums <https://forums.cacti.net/>.

Cacti 1.2 installations, use the 1.2.x manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/tree/1.2.x>. Menu visibility

show an earlier release or a different theme.

Current Manuals and Contributed Archives


These replacements follow develop, the unreleased Cacti 1.3 series. Use
the 1.2.x manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/1.2.x/README.md> for released Cacti 1.2. The version guidance follows
the website's distinction between released and development documentation.
The manuals reuse the maintained chapter content and existing documentation
styles. They do not import the website's visual design.


Complete maintained manuals


* PDF manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/pdf/cacti-manual.pdf>, with all current main chapters,
bookmarks, screenshots, and links between chapters.

* UTF-8 text manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/manuals/manual.txt>, with the same main chapters,
command examples, image descriptions, and full link destinations.

* HTML manual publication <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resources/build/HTML-Manual.md>, including all
chapters and legacy chapter URLs.

* Historical ReportIt manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/pdf/manual_reportit.pdf>. The newer
plugin manual is maintained separately from these Cacti editions.



Current replacements for contributed guides


The new guides are separate Cacti documentation, not revisions attributed to
the original contributors. German, Turkish, and Russian editions contain a
new localized introduction and complete relevant English chapters in their
PDF and text appendices. They are not full translations. Language review is
welcome; platform checks and runtime limitations still apply.





	Guide
	Read online
	PDF
	Text

The German and Turkish appendices include every current main manual chapter.

troubleshooting, security, and upgrade chapters selected in
editions.json <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resources/manuals/editions.json>. Solaris has a migration
guide rather than an unverified new-install procedure. The Windows guides
explain the current development dependency restriction before any install
instructions.


The schema SQL <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/manuals/cacti-schema.sql> contains only the pinned
upstream CREATE TABLE statements. It is a reference, not an installer or
upgrade script. It has no seed data and does not describe plugin tables.
Use the schema supplied with your exact Cacti release for installation.

Build and publish


Follow manual publication <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resources/build/Other-Manuals.md> to rebuild and
validate every edition. The edition manifest <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/manuals/build-manifest.json>
records exact sources and checksums. The PDF builder also records its locked
toolchain in the PDF manifest <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/resource/pdf/build_manifest.json>.


CI publishes review artifacts; it does not deploy the public file host.
Maintainers should publish the new editions under distinct versioned names,
link this index from the download page, and retain the original archive URLs.
Publish the main manual separately from the historic manual.pdf and
manual.txt names so an existing archive citation keeps its meaning.

Requirements


This manual follows the unreleased Cacti 1.3 develop branch. Use the
1.2.x manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/tree/1.2.x> for released Cacti 1.2 installations. Verify the
requirements of the exact revision or release you deploy; development
requirements can change.


Supported operating systems


Platform guidance checked on 26 October 2026. Use the current security-updated
point release in a vendor-supported stable branch. The examples below target
these maintained operating systems; vendor OS support does not certify every
Cacti, PHP, database, or third-party binary combination.





	Operating system
	Versions used for installation guidance
	Lifecycle source






	RHEL
	Current supported minor release of 9 or 10
	Red Hat lifecycle <https://access.redhat.com/support/policy/updates/errata>




	Rocky Linux
	Current minor release of 9 or 10
	Rocky releases <https://docs.rockylinux.org/releases/>




	AlmaLinux
	Current minor release of 9 or 10
	AlmaLinux releases <https://wiki.almalinux.org/release-notes/>




	Ubuntu Server
	24.04 LTS, 26.04 LTS, or 26.10
	Ubuntu lifecycle <https://ubuntu.com/about/release-cycle>




	Debian
	13 stable; 12 only with applicable Debian LTS coverage
	Debian lifecycle <https://www.debian.org/releases/>




	FreeBSD
	14.5-RELEASE or 15.1-RELEASE, with current patches
	FreeBSD security support <https://www.freebsd.org/security/>




	Windows Server
	2022 or 2025, with current updates
	Server 2022 <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/lifecycle/products/windows-server-2022>, Server 2025 <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/lifecycle/products/windows-server-2025>





Check the linked lifecycle information before deployment. LTS coverage can
exclude architectures or packages. Use a maintained PHP and database version
that also meets this Cacti branch's requirements. A supported OS with an
incompatible default PHP version is not ready for installation. Do not use a
beta OS or an expired point release for these procedures.


Software Requirements





	Component
	Development requirement or recommendation






	Web server
	PHP-capable Apache, nginx, or IIS FastCGI




	PHP
	PHP 8.3 or later in the PHP 8 series (^8.3 in Composer)




	Database
	MySQL 8.4+ or MariaDB 10.11+ recommended by the installer




	RRDtool
	Version 1.5 or later




	SNMP
	Net-SNMP command-line tools for operations using external binaries




	Spine
	Optional; use a version compatible with Cacti and its runtime libraries





The PHP requirement comes from the dependency manifest <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/composer.json> and
runtime minimum <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/include/global_constants.php>. The database values are the branch's
installer recommendations <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/lib/utility.php>, rather than a certification of every
vendor release. Check the actual server and extensions before installation.


PHP extensions


Cacti checks the CLI and web PHP environments separately. Install the
extensions required by the selected revision's Composer manifest and lock file,
and satisfy the installer checks. The combined requirements include:


* ctype, curl, date, dom, filter, gd, gmp, hash, intl,
json, ldap, mbstring, mysqlnd, openssl, pcre, PDO,
pdo_mysql, Phar, session, simplexml, sockets, spl, sqlite3,
standard, xml, and zlib.

* posix and CLI pcntl on Unix.

* com_dotnet for the Windows installer checks.



Some modules are built into PHP. Check php -v, php --ini, and php -m for
the CLI, then check web PHP through the installer. Enabling an extension in one
PHP configuration does not enable it in the other.


The PHP SNMP extension is optional. External Net-SNMP tools are still needed
for collection paths such as some SNMPv3 and bulk requests. Configure their
paths and verify execution under the actual web and collector accounts.


Composer and source deployments


A source checkout or GitHub source archive needs its locked PHP dependencies.
Run these commands from the Cacti source root as the deployment owner:


composer install --no-dev
composer check-platform-reqs --no-dev



Use composer install, which reads composer.lock. Do not run composer update during installation or upgrade. A complete release bundle can already
contain include/vendor/autoload.php; verify its generated platform checks and
the release's instructions rather than assuming every archive contains vendor
files.


The current manifest requires ext-pcntl and ext-posix unconditionally.
Native Windows PHP cannot supply these modules. Source dependency installation
is therefore blocked on native Windows, even though the installer has
Windows-specific checks. A bundled artifact must also pass its runtime platform
check. See Installing Under Windows <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Installing-Under-Windows.md#development-packaging-prerequisite> before choosing a build. Do not
bypass platform checks to force installation.


Hardware Sizing


Size from the polling interval, number of data sources, retention profiles,
concurrent graph requests, collector settings, and measured storage latency.
Device count alone is not a reliable sizing rule. Test representative polling
and graphing load before increasing the deployment.


Use storage that can sustain RRD updates and database writes within the polling
window. Measure CPU, memory, I/O latency, database connections, and poll
completion time. Remote collectors can distribute collection work; they also
require connectivity, synchronization, and operational monitoring. Spine
process and thread counts should be tuned against those measurements.


Database configuration


Enable timezone support and grant the application account SELECT access to
mysql.time_zone_name. The grant does not populate that table. Import the
vendor's timezone data using the procedure for your operating system.


Use utf8mb4 and the utf8mb4_unicode_ci collation consistently for the Cacti
database. Changing server defaults does not convert existing tables; back up
and plan any conversion of an installed database separately.


Start with the database vendor's defaults and review the installer's checks for
the installed engine and version. Do not copy a fixed my.cnf block from
another server. Size global buffers and per-connection buffers together with
PHP and operating-system memory. Estimate Spine's connection demand as:


total_connections = total_processes * (total_threads + script_servers + 1)



Allow additional connections for web requests, plugins, and maintenance.
Inspect actual storage engines before applying memory-table sizing guidance.


Keep InnoDB doublewrite protection enabled. Disabling it removes protection
against incomplete page writes; an SSD alone does not establish atomic page
writes. Review the durability consequences before changing commit flushing or
other crash-recovery settings.


Database variables differ by engine and release. Removed settings can prevent
startup. For example, MySQL 8 no longer accepts innodb_file_format,
innodb_large_prefix, or innodb_additional_mem_pool_size, and MariaDB 10.6
removed innodb_buffer_pool_instances. Validate settings against the installed
server's documentation and Cacti's version-aware checks before restarting. See
the MySQL reference <https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/innodb-parameters.html> or MariaDB reference <https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-usage/storage-engines/innodb/innodb-system-variables>.


Accounts and platform controls


Keep application code owned by the administrator or deployment account. Give
web and collector identities the read, execute, and runtime-directory write
access they need. Grant temporary installation or import access only to the
required paths, and remove it afterward. Keep database credentials, CSRF
secrets, private caches, RRD files, and backups inaccessible over HTTP.


Configure SELinux, AppArmor, container permissions, or IIS request filtering
for the required operations. Do not remove confinement globally to make
installation checks pass. Follow the platform installation guide and
Hardening a Cacti Installation <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Hardening-Cacti-Installation.md>.




Copyright (c) 2004-2026 The Cacti Group


General Installing Instructions


Use only the supported operating systems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md#supported-operating-systems> and maintained
dependencies compatible with this Cacti branch.


Install the packages required by the selected operating system and verify that
the configured web server, PHP handler, and database start at boot.


Containers


Use an unprivileged container with its normal security profile where possible.
Diagnose the specific ICMP, socket, or filesystem permission that fails and
adjust only the required capability or policy. Do not remove AppArmor
confinement or make the entire container privileged as a general prerequisite.


Required Packages for Most Operating Systems


Depending on your operating system and PHP version, certain packages are
required for Cacti. The largest variable in these requirements come with regard
to PHP and MySQL/MariaDB.


Installation requirements include the packages below. The installation of these
packages will vary by operating system.


Base OS


* apache, IIS, or nginx


* net-snmp, net-snmp-utils


* rrdtool


* help2man (for spine)


* dos2unix (for spine)


* development packages (gcc, automake, autoconf, libtool, either mysql-devel or
mariadb-devel, net-snmp-devel, help2man)


(for spine)




Database


Use the database versions and configuration described in
Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md>. The development branch recommends MySQL 8.4+
or MariaDB 10.11+. Check the installed server with the Cacti installer.


* mysql


* mysql-server


* libmysqlclient




or


* mariadb


* mariadb-server


* libmariadbclient




PHP Modules


Use PHP 8.3 or later in the PHP 8 series and the extensions listed in
Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md>. Verify the CLI with php -v and php -m and
check web PHP separately in the installer. Source deployments also need
Composer and all extensions required by the selected revision's lock file.
Run composer install --no-dev as the deployment owner, then
composer check-platform-reqs --no-dev. Use the lock file rather than updating
dependencies during installation.


Systems using PHP-FPM


After changing PHP configuration, test and restart the actual FPM service.
Service names can be versioned, for example on Debian and Ubuntu. Check the
installed unit rather than assuming that php-fpm is the service name. Confirm
its socket path and permissions match the web-server handler.


FreeBSD


Use a patched release listed in supported operating systems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md#supported-operating-systems>.
Choose binary packages or the Ports Collection using the
FreeBSD Handbook <https://docs.freebsd.org/en/books/handbook/ports/>. Run package and service administration
commands as root. Use the dedicated poller account for collection.


Choose the Cacti version and PHP flavor


The FreeBSD net-mgmt/cacti port currently packages Cacti 1.2.31, not the
unreleased Cacti 1.3 development branch covered by this manual. For a packaged
release, follow its installed instructions and the 1.2.x manual <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/tree/1.2.x>.
For development source, meet Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md> and follow the
source-install steps below using FreeBSD paths and service configuration.
Do not overlay a source checkout onto files owned by pkg.


Cacti packages have PHP flavors. Discover the names available in your
configured repository before selecting one:


pkg update
pkg search -S name -x '^cacti(-php[0-9]+)?$'



For example, if the repository offers cacti-php84 and the installation uses
PHP 8.4, review and install that flavor:


pkg install -n cacti-php84
pkg install cacti-php84
pkg info -D cacti-php84
pkg info -l cacti-php84



Replace the example name with the selected flavor. Read the transaction before
accepting it, particularly changes to PHP extensions and database clients.
Dependencies do not configure a web server or create a database server. Set up
a compatible local MariaDB/MySQL service, or an existing remote database,
and configure Apache or nginx with the matching PHP handler separately.
Use FreeBSD service and sysrc for installed rc.d services; Linux
systemctl examples do not apply.


Build from ports when custom options are required


Use a maintained ports tree and read /usr/ports/UPDATING. Clone a tree only
if /usr/ports does not already contain one:


pkg install git
# New tree only:
git clone --depth 1 https://git.FreeBSD.org/ports.git /usr/ports
# Existing Git tree instead:
git -C /usr/ports pull --ff-only



Build the chosen flavor, for example:


cd /usr/ports/net-mgmt/cacti
make FLAVOR=php84 config
make FLAVOR=php84 install clean



The supported flavors and dependencies come from that ports revision. Follow
the handbook's dependency-management guidance before mixing custom ports with
binary packages. Rebuilding a port does not turn the packaged release into
Cacti development source.


Check installed paths and services


The Cacti port <https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-ports/tree/main/net-mgmt/cacti> uses these default locations. Custom ports
options and existing Cacti settings can change them; inspect the installed
files and application configuration before copying commands.





	Item
	Default location or account






	Application and bundled schema
	/usr/local/share/cacti




	Database configuration
	/usr/local/share/cacti/include/config.php




	PHP configuration
	/usr/local/etc/php.ini




	RRD data
	/var/db/cacti/rra




	Log directory
	/var/log/cacti




	PHP CLI
	/usr/local/bin/php




	Packaged poller account
	cacti




	Web-server account
	Commonly www; verify the service configuration





The port supplies include/config.php.sample; preserve an existing
include/config.php and use the sample when creating a new one. Set the
intended timezone in both CLI and web PHP. Initialize a new database from the
bundled cacti.sql, grant the application account access to its database and
mysql.time_zone_name, and verify database timezone data is loaded. Never
import cacti.sql over an existing installation.


Check package-specific patches as well as upstream instructions. For example,
the current port sets $php_snmp_support = false for new configurations;
verify the installed setting and Net-SNMP paths when troubleshooting polling.


Configure write access only for the runtime directories needed by the actual
poller and web processes. Keep application code and database credentials
protected. Preserve Cacti's web-server restrictions on configuration, CLI,
resource, and data directories.


Create one Cacti schedule. For a five-minute launcher, an example entry in the
cacti user's crontab is:


*/5 * * * * /usr/local/bin/php -q /usr/local/share/cacti/poller.php



Match this cadence to Cron/Daemon Interval in Cacti. A system crontab needs
the account field as well. Check for an existing cron entry or daemon first;
do not run both. Use Data Source Debugging <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Data-Debug.md> and the Cacti log
to verify collection after completing the web installer.


Optional Spine poller


The Spine port <https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-ports/tree/main/net-mgmt/cacti-spine> is net-mgmt/cacti-spine; its package is
spine. Install it only when its version matches the Cacti release you use:


pkg install -n spine
pkg install spine



Its default binary is /usr/local/bin/spine, with
/usr/local/etc/spine.conf.sample as the configuration example. Supply the
correct database connection in spine.conf and select the binary in Cacti.
Verify the poller account can run it. Grant ICMP privileges only when the
selected polling method requires them; do not apply a blanket setuid command.


For an existing installation, follow
Upgrading Under FreeBSD <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Upgrading-Cacti-Under-FreeBSD.md>.


Configure PHP


Verify that the modules are installed and configured correctly. There are
several ways to do so - consult
PHP configuration instructions <https://www.php.net/manual/en/configuration.php>
for a complete description.


It is imperative that you set the date.timezone in your /etc/php.ini, or
/etc/phpX/apache/php.ini and /etc/phpX/cli/php.ini files. Failure to do so
will result in errors after the install is complete.


Most other PHP configuration is done automatically by the base OS, so there is
no need to discuss that here.


Configure the Webserver (Apache)


Use the distribution's Apache/PHP integration, selecting either its PHP-FPM
handler or a compatible Apache PHP module. Do not configure both handlers for
the same files. The Enterprise Linux LAMP guide <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Install-Under-Enterprise-Linux-LAMP.md> and
Ubuntu/Debian guide <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Installing-Under-Ubuntu-Debian.md> provide examples.


Verify Apache's configuration before reloading. Restrict access during setup,
use HTTPS, and ensure that PHP requests execute rather than expose source. Keep
Cacti's shipped .htaccess restrictions active, or reproduce them in the
server configuration if overrides are disabled. nginx and IIS need their own
restrictions; they do not interpret Apache .htaccess files.


Configure MySQL/MariaDB


Initialize and secure the database using the procedure for the installed MySQL
or MariaDB package. Use the administrator authentication method configured by
that package; some Unix packages use local socket authentication. Store
credentials securely and do not put passwords directly on shell command lines.


You must also load timezone information into the database. This is required for
various plugin use. Later, you will be required to grant access to the
time_zone_name table during the final installation steps.


mysql_tzinfo_to_sql /usr/share/zoneinfo | mysql -u root -p mysql



Since Cacti 1.x is supporting internationalization (i18n), it is important that
the default character set for MySQL/MariaDB be i18n compatible. The Cacti
installer will make specific recommendations on MySQL/MariaDB settings. Follow
those as applicable for your OS.


Galera clustering: There are several tables which are set to use the MEMORY
storage engine which do not get replicated among nodes which can cause
problems. If you configure Cacti to only connect to one node of your cluster
and are not load balancing this does not apply to you.


If you are running multiple nodes in a load-balanced environment where you
connect to a VIP you should remove all but one node from rotation during Cacti
installation or update. After the installation/update login to your MySQL
server and execute the following commands to update those tables to use the
InnoDB engine:


MariaDB [(none)]> use cacti;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `automation_ips` ENGINE=InnoDB;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `automation_processes` ENGINE=InnoDB;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `data_source_stats_hourly_cache` ENGINE=InnoDB;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `data_source_stats_hourly_last` ENGINE=InnoDB;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `poller_output` ENGINE=InnoDB;
MariaDB [cacti]> ALTER TABLE `poller_output_boost_processes` ENGINE=InnoDB;



Before restoring database load balancing, verify the table-engine changes,
replication state and successful polling on the configured topology. A fixed
number of polling cycles is not proof that the cluster is ready.


Install and Configure Cacti


These are fresh source-install examples for Cacti development (1.3).
Package-managed installations should use the maintainer's procedure instead.
For an existing installation, follow Upgrading Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Upgrading-Cacti.md>. The
examples use /var/www/html/cacti; adjust that path and the service accounts
for your OS. Use administrator privileges for software installation and
permission changes.


Extract the source and create the database


Obtain the intended source revision in a new installation directory. Do not run
these commands over an existing deployment:


git clone --branch develop --depth 1 https://github.com/Cacti/cacti.git cacti
mv cacti /var/www/html/cacti
cd /var/www/html/cacti
composer install --no-dev
composer check-platform-reqs --no-dev



The installation root should now contain cacti.sql, poller.php and
include/config.php.dist, rather than another nested release directory.


Connect with a database administrator account:


mysql --user=root --password



Create the database and a dedicated application account. Replace the password
placeholder before running this SQL, and use the same secret in the Cacti
configuration. The database account is separate from the operating-system
account that runs the poller.


CREATE DATABASE cacti CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
CREATE USER 'cactiuser'@'localhost'
  IDENTIFIED BY 'REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD';
GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON cacti.* TO 'cactiuser'@'localhost';
GRANT SELECT ON mysql.time_zone_name TO 'cactiuser'@'localhost';
EXIT;



Use an authorized database administrator to grant access. Do not edit
mysql.tables_priv as a substitute for an authorized GRANT operation. Ensure
the server's timezone tables are populated using the procedure for the
installed MySQL or MariaDB server; granting access does not populate them.


Import the schema into the new database using the appropriate client:


mysql --user=root --password --default-character-set=utf8mb4 cacti \
  < /var/www/html/cacti/cacti.sql



The --password option prompts for the password. Use mariadb in place of
mysql where appropriate for the installed server.


Configure access and complete installation


Copy the supplied configuration example:


cp /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php.dist \
  /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php



Edit the copied file to match the account created above:


$database_type     = 'mysql';
$database_default  = 'cacti';
$database_hostname = 'localhost';
$database_username = 'cactiuser';
$database_password = 'REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD';
$database_port     = '3306';
$url_path          = '/cacti/';



Identify the actual web and poller service accounts. Give them the required
read/execute access to application files and external tools, and write access
to the runtime paths they use, including RRD, log and cache paths. They need
not be the same account. Do not assume a database username is also a local OS
account or make the whole application tree writable.


During installation and package imports, grant the performing identity the
additional access required by the installer, including query/script resources
and the location used to initialize the CSRF secret. Remove temporary setup
permissions afterward. Preserve read access to the generated secret; do not
create an empty secret file. See the platform-specific installation guides for
their account and permission examples.


Restrict installer access to the administrator and use HTTPS or a protected
local connection before entering credentials. A freshly imported development
schema uses application username admin and initial password admin. Open the
configured Cacti URL, sign in and choose a unique administrator password when
prompted. These are separate from the database credentials. Complete the
installer's dependency, database, path and permission checks before allowing
general access.


Schedule collection after installation


Use either cron or cactid.service, not both. The following examples assume an
existing local user and group named cacti with the required polling
permissions; replace them with the actual service identity for your system. Do
not start polling before completing the web installer.


For a system cron file such as /etc/cron.d/cacti, use an absolute PHP CLI
path and include the OS username:


*/5 * * * * cacti /usr/bin/php /var/www/html/cacti/poller.php >/dev/null 2>&1



The five-minute launch interval must match Cron/Daemon Interval under the
poller settings. This is separate from individual data-source polling
intervals. The redirection above works with /bin/sh; &> is Bash-specific.


The supplied systemd unit uses Type=forking. Confirm that the selected PHP
CLI provides pcntl_fork before using it; otherwise use cron or install the
appropriate PCNTL support. For the example CLI path, this should report
bool(true):


/usr/bin/php -r 'var_dump(function_exists("pcntl_fork"));'



For systemd, copy the supplied unit and edit the installed copy:


cp /var/www/html/cacti/service/cactid.service \
  /etc/systemd/system/cactid.service



Replace the unit's existing User, Group, ExecStart and EnvironmentFile
settings with values for the actual deployment, retaining its other settings.
For example:


User=cacti
Group=cacti
ExecStart=/usr/bin/php /var/www/html/cacti/cactid.php
EnvironmentFile=-/etc/default/cactid



The - allows the optional environment file to be absent. Disable the
Cacti-specific cron entry before enabling the daemon, then load the edited
unit:


systemctl daemon-reload
systemctl enable cactid.service
systemctl start cactid.service
systemctl status cactid.service



The unit manages the local daemon; it does not establish high availability.
Verify scheduler execution, collector status, logs and recent graph data under
the configured account.


(Optional) Install and Configure Spine


Spine is a very fast data collection engine, written in C. It is an optional
replacement for cmd.php. If you decide to use it, you will have to install it
explicitly. It does not come with cacti itself.


The easiest way is to install Spine using rpm or ports. You will find packages
for Spine at the main cacti site or from your distribution.


To compile Spine, download it to any location of your liking. Then, issue from
the downloaded directory following commands


./bootstrap



After bootstrap succeeds, follow the release's build instructions to
configure, compile and install Spine.


Assuming, you've managed to install spine correctly, you will have to configure
it. Use the installed spine.conf.dist as the example, and configure the
actual Spine configuration path in Cacti. Use the same database account and
secret as the Cacti configuration, and restrict file access to the poller
account and administrators.


DB_Host     localhost
DB_Database cacti
DB_User     cactiuser
DB_Pass     REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD
DB_Port     3306



Considerations when using Proxies in front of Cacti (Cacti 1.2.23+)


A header allowlist does not authenticate a proxy. Trust forwarded client-IP
headers only when a trusted proxy replaces those headers and untrusted clients
cannot bypass it to reach Cacti directly. Leave forwarding disabled otherwise.
In include/config.php, select only the headers your proxy sets. For a
deployment using X-Forwarded-For, the setting is:


$proxy_headers = ['X-Forwarded-For'];

Installing on Enterprise Linux with Apache


Use only the supported operating systems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md#supported-operating-systems> and maintained
dependencies compatible with this Cacti branch.


This source-install example covers the unreleased Cacti 1.3 development branch
on RHEL, Rocky Linux, or AlmaLinux. Read Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md>
first. Run administrative commands as root and adapt paths and accounts to
your deployment. For an existing installation, use
Upgrading Cacti <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Upgrading-Cacti.md>. For package-managed Cacti, follow the
package maintainer's procedure instead of overlaying source files.


Choose repositories and PHP


For a new, fully updated RHEL 9 installation with the stream available,
PHP 8.3 <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/9/html/9.6_release_notes/new-features> is an example meeting the development minimum:


dnf module list php
dnf module install php:8.3



This is a fresh-install example, not a stream-switching procedure for an
existing server. Check the streams supplied by your distribution. EL 9 and EL
10 do not share identical repositories or module availability. If the required
PHP version is unavailable, choose a maintained distribution or repository that
provides it before continuing.


Install the web and database packages


This example uses MariaDB. MySQL installations should use the package and
service names for their selected MySQL repository. Review the database version
against Cacti's requirements before installation.


dnf install -y httpd php-fpm php-cli php-mysqlnd php-gd php-gmp \
  php-intl php-ldap php-mbstring php-xml php-process php-snmp \
  mariadb-server rrdtool net-snmp-utils cronie
php -v
php -m



Additional packages can be required by the chosen source revision. Check
Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md>, including Composer dependencies where
applicable. Do not mix extensions from different PHP versions or repositories.


Configure date.timezone in the PHP configuration used by both CLI and FPM.
Check php --ini for CLI and the installer for web PHP. Review the FPM pool at
/etc/php-fpm.d/www.conf; its account needs access to Cacti's runtime paths.
Keep code owned by the deployment administrator.


Start the database:


systemctl enable --now mariadb



Follow the installed database vendor's initialization and account-security
procedure. Do not set a root password on the shell command line. Configure only
variables accepted by that database version; use Cacti's installer
recommendations rather than a fixed tuning block. Keep InnoDB doublewrite
protection enabled.


Configure Apache


Use the distribution's Apache/PHP-FPM integration. Check that PHP requests are
executed by FPM rather than served as text. This example exposes only
/cacti/; replace the hostname and certificate paths before use. Save it in
/etc/httpd/conf.d/cacti.conf:


<VirtualHost *:443>
    ServerName cacti.example.com
    SSLEngine On
    SSLCertificateFile /etc/pki/tls/certs/cacti.crt
    SSLCertificateKeyFile /etc/pki/tls/private/cacti.key
    Alias /cacti /var/www/html/cacti

    <Directory /var/www/html/cacti>
        Options -Indexes
        AllowOverride All
        Require all granted
        DirectoryIndex index.php
    </Directory>
</VirtualHost>



Install mod_ssl if not already present. AllowOverride All lets Cacti's
shipped .htaccess restrictions protect private paths; if your policy disables
overrides, reproduce those restrictions in the server configuration. Do not
grant general access to private directories as a troubleshooting step.


dnf install -y mod_ssl
httpd -t
systemctl enable --now php-fpm httpd



Use your certificate-management procedure and review any packaged default TLS
virtual host for conflicts. Restrict access to administrators during setup.


Install and configure Cacti


Use General Installing Instructions <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/General-Installing-Instructions.md#install-and-configure-cacti> to obtain the intended source,
create a new database and dedicated application account, load timezone data,
import cacti.sql, and configure include/config.php. The examples there use
/var/www/html/cacti; use that same path here.


Complete the dependency and filesystem-permission checks before opening the
installer. The database account and operating-system service accounts are
separate identities. Grant runtime write access to the actual FPM and poller
accounts, not automatically to the nginx master process or the database user.
Keep executable code and credentials protected during normal operation.


Open https://cacti.example.com/cacti/ and complete the installer. Configure
one scheduler after installation: either a Cacti-specific cron entry or the
supplied cactid systemd service, using the procedure in the general guide. Do
not run both for the same collector. The five-minute cron example is:


*/5 * * * * apache /usr/bin/php /PATH/TO/cacti/poller.php >/dev/null 2>&1



Replace /PATH/TO/cacti and apache with the installed path and chosen poller
account. Enable crond if using cron. Verify a manual poll under that account,
then check scheduled runs, logs, collector status, and fresh graph samples.


SELinux and containers


Keep SELinux enforcing. For a custom installation path, use persistent file
contexts and allow writes only on Cacti's runtime paths. Review audit denials
and authorize the specific network and executable access needed by FPM,
RRDtool, SNMP tools, and the collector. Do not use setenforce 0 as the
installation procedure. See the RHEL SELinux guide <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/9/html/using_selinux/index>.


For LXC or Proxmox, retain an unprivileged container and its confinement where
possible. Diagnose the specific ICMP/socket permission or mapped-file access
failure. Adjust only the required capability or policy; do not make the entire
container privileged or set an unconfined AppArmor profile merely to run Cacti.


Optional Spine and proxies


Follow Spine <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Spine.md> for the optional compiled collector. Select a
compatible source or package and the development headers for your OS.
Repository names differ: do not apply Rocky/AlmaLinux crb commands to RHEL
without checking its repository configuration. Configure the Spine database
connection with the same application credentials and restrict access to its
configuration file.


When using a reverse proxy, see the proxy configuration in the general
guide <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/General-Installing-Instructions.md>. A header allowlist alone does not authenticate a proxy; prevent
clients from bypassing the trusted proxy and require it to replace forwarded
client-IP headers.




Copyright (c) 2004-2026 The Cacti Group


Installing Cacti Development on Ubuntu or Debian with Apache


For nginx with PHP-FPM on Ubuntu Server 26.04 LTS or 26.10, use the
Ubuntu LEMP guide <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Installing-Under-Ubuntu-LEMP.md>.


Use only the supported operating systems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md#supported-operating-systems> and maintained
dependencies compatible with this Cacti branch.


This is a source-install example for Cacti development (1.3), not a tested
distribution
matrix. Use a maintained distribution and check its PHP version against
Requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md> before
installing packages. Run the administrative commands below as root. The
example uses /var/www/html/cacti and the default Debian/Ubuntu web account,
www-data; adjust both if your web-server setup differs.


Install the required packages


The unversioned PHP package names select the distribution's configured PHP
version. Confirm that it is PHP 8.3 or later in the PHP 8 series before
continuing. Use a distribution or configured repository that supplies the
required version; do not mix extensions from different PHP versions.


apt-get update
apt-get install -y apache2 libapache2-mod-php mariadb-server rrdtool snmp git cron \
  composer php-cli php-curl php-sqlite3 php-mysql php-snmp php-xml php-mbstring php-gd php-gmp php-zip php-ldap php-intl
systemctl enable --now apache2 mariadb cron



If using PHP-FPM instead of the Apache PHP module, configure Apache's FPM
integration and use the installed FPM pool's account. FPM service names are
versioned on Debian/Ubuntu; inspect them with systemctl list-unit-files 'php*-fpm.service' and restart the actual unit after configuration changes. Do
not assume a service named php-fpm exists.


Download the Cacti source


This example checks out the unreleased develop branch. Record the commit you
deploy; do not treat a moving development branch as a stable release.


git clone --branch develop --depth 1 https://github.com/Cacti/cacti.git cacti
mv cacti /var/www/html/cacti
cd /var/www/html/cacti
composer install --no-dev
composer check-platform-reqs --no-dev



Do not overwrite an existing installation with these fresh-install commands.


Create the database


Choose a unique database password and replace the example placeholder in both
the SQL and include/config.php. Connect using your database administrator's
authentication method; the example below prompts for a password.


mysql -u root -p



CREATE DATABASE cacti CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
CREATE USER 'cactiuser'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED BY 'replace-with-a-unique-password';
GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON cacti.* TO 'cactiuser'@'localhost';
GRANT SELECT ON mysql.time_zone_name TO 'cactiuser'@'localhost';
EXIT;



Load the system timezone data and the schema into this new database. A MariaDB
installation may provide mariadb-tzinfo-to-sql as the timezone utility's
name.


mysql_tzinfo_to_sql /usr/share/zoneinfo | mysql -u root -p mysql
mysql -u root -p cacti < /var/www/html/cacti/cacti.sql



Configure Cacti and filesystem permissions


Copy the distributed configuration example; do not rename or delete it.


cp /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php.dist /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php



Edit the database fields to match the account and database created above:


$database_type     = 'mysql';
$database_default  = 'cacti';
$database_hostname = 'localhost';
$database_username = 'cactiuser';
$database_password = 'replace-with-a-unique-password';
$database_port     = '3306';



Keep application code owned by the administrator. The web and poller accounts
must be able to read the configuration and write the runtime directories:


chown root:www-data /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php
chmod 640 /var/www/html/cacti/include/config.php
chown -R www-data:www-data /var/www/html/cacti/rra /var/www/html/cacti/log /var/www/html/cacti/cache
chmod -R u+rwX,go-rwx /var/www/html/cacti/rra /var/www/html/cacti/log /var/www/html/cacti/cache



During installation or package import, the installer also checks write access
to resource/snmp_queries, resource/script_server, resource/script_queries
and scripts, and the location used to create the CSRF secret. Grant only the
additional access requested by the installer and remove that temporary access
afterwards. Do not make the entire application tree world-writable.


Restrict installer access to the administrator. Configure HTTPS or use a
protected local connection before entering credentials, then browse to
/cacti/. For a freshly imported development schema, sign in with application
username admin and initial password admin, then choose a unique
administrator password when prompted. These are separate from the database
credentials. Complete the web installer before starting scheduled polling or
allowing general access.


Schedule polling


Choose either cron or cactid.service, not both. Complete the web installer
before starting the selected scheduler. The example cron interval is five
minutes; match the Cron/Daemon Interval in Settings > Poller. This is
separate from the data-source polling interval, which can be shorter.


Cron


Create /etc/cron.d/cacti as root, with mode 644, and include this line:


*/5 * * * * www-data /usr/bin/php /var/www/html/cacti/poller.php >/dev/null 2>&1



This is a system cron file, so the username field is required. Use an absolute
PHP CLI path and POSIX shell redirection; Debian cron uses /bin/sh by
default. See Debian crontab format <https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/cron/crontab.5.en.html>.


systemd


The supplied unit uses Type=forking, so the selected PHP CLI must provide
pcntl_fork. Check /usr/bin/php -r 'var_dump(function_exists("pcntl_fork"));' for bool(true) before using this
example. If fork support is unavailable, use cron or install the appropriate
PCNTL support for that PHP CLI.


Copy the supplied service and edit the installed copy:


cp /var/www/html/cacti/service/cactid.service /etc/systemd/system/cactid.service



Replace its existing User, Group, EnvironmentFile and ExecStart entries
with the following, retaining the other service settings:


User=www-data
Group=www-data
EnvironmentFile=-/etc/default/cactid
ExecStart=/usr/bin/php /var/www/html/cacti/cactid.php



The leading - makes the optional environment file unnecessary if you do not
use one. After completing the installer, load and start the service:


systemctl daemon-reload
systemctl enable cactid.service
systemctl start cactid.service
systemctl status cactid.service



The service manages the local polling daemon; it does not by itself provide
high availability. Verify the poller log and newly created graphs after
starting the selected scheduler.


Considerations when using Proxies in front of Cacti (Cacti 1.2.23+)


A header allowlist does not authenticate a proxy. Trust forwarded client-IP
headers only when a trusted proxy replaces those headers and untrusted clients
cannot bypass it to reach Cacti directly. Leave forwarding disabled otherwise.
In include/config.php, select only the headers your proxy sets. For a
deployment using X-Forwarded-For, the setting is:


$proxy_headers = ['X-Forwarded-For'];

Installing Under Windows


Use only the supported operating systems <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md#supported-operating-systems> and maintained
dependencies compatible with this Cacti branch.


These instructions describe a manual installation of Cacti development (1.3) on
Windows. Use the requirements <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Requirements.md> for your Cacti release and
supported Windows builds of PHP, your database server and RRDtool. Community
Windows installers are also discussed in the Cacti
forums <https://forums.cacti.net/>; check their included versions and
maintenance status before using them.


The examples use C:\inetpub\wwwroot\cacti for Cacti, C:\php for PHP,
C:\rrdtool for RRDtool and C:\spine for optional Spine. Substitute your
actual paths throughout. Use a dedicated Windows account for the poller and a
separate web-server identity. Install software and configure services as an
administrator; the poller does not normally need administrator privileges.


Development packaging prerequisite


The current development dependency manifest requires PHP ^8.3, including
ext-pcntl and ext-posix unconditionally. Native Windows PHP cannot supply
those two extensions. A source checkout therefore cannot currently install its
Composer dependencies on native Windows. A bundled artifact must also pass its
generated runtime platform check. Stop if it rejects Windows and use a
compatible release or a Unix installation. Do not bypass platform checks or
edit generated vendor files to force an incompatible installation.


The remaining instructions apply only to an artifact that passes its platform
checks. Read the PHP PCNTL platform restriction <https://www.php.net/manual/en/pcntl.installation.php> and the selected
revision's dependency manifest <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/composer.json> before choosing an artifact.


Install the software


* Choose IIS or Apache and install only the web server you intend to use on
the selected address and port.


* Extract the Cacti ZIP distribution into the chosen web directory.


* Install PHP and its required runtime libraries using the
PHP Windows installation instructions <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.manual.php>. Match the architecture
and build type to your web-server integration. Use a Non-Thread-Safe build
for IIS FastCGI; an Apache module requires a compatible Thread-Safe build.
A path without spaces, such as C:\php, simplifies tool configuration.


* Install a Windows build of RRDtool <https://oss.oetiker.ch/rrdtool/> and
verify its runtime dependencies. If using Spine, install a compatible
Spine release <https://github.com/Cacti/documentation/blob/67bc24e44000/Spine.md> and the dependencies supplied or required by that
build. The PHP poller can be used without Spine.


* Install MySQL or MariaDB. Use the server's supported Windows configuration
and service-management instructions. Start with its documented defaults,
then review the Cacti installer's checks for your actual server and
workload.


Do not copy a tuning block from a different database version or operating
system. MySQL 8 removed NO_AUTO_CREATE_USER, innodb_file_format and
innodb_large_prefix; these settings can prevent startup. Do not disable
InnoDB doublewrite protection or reduce commit durability as an installation
shortcut. Size global and per-connection buffers for the available memory
and expected concurrency.


* Install Net-SNMP command-line tools if your collection configuration needs
them, for example into C:\usr. Cacti uses these tools for SNMPv3
requests from the web interface and PHP poller; PHP's SNMP extension alone
does not provide that collection path. Verify the installed executable paths
instead of assuming a particular short filename or installation directory.




Configure PHP


Copy php.ini-production from the PHP distribution to C:\php\php.ini. Set
the timezone for your installation and enable the required extensions. For
current PHP Windows builds, an example configuration is:


extension_dir = "C:\php\ext"
extension=curl
extension=pdo_mysql
extension=sqlite3
extension=gd
extension=gmp
extension=intl
extension=ldap
extension=mbstring
extension=openssl
extension=sockets
extension=com_dotnet
; Enable if using PHP's SNMP extension:
extension=snmp

date.timezone = America/Detroit
file_uploads = On



Use only extensions supplied for the selected PHP build and install their
required DLL dependencies according to PHP's instructions. Some modules are
built in. The Cacti installer checks additional modules, including SimpleXML,
XML and zlib, in both the web and command-line environments. pdo_mysql is the
database driver Cacti uses; the removed php_mysql.dll is not a substitute.
Set memory and execution limits using the installer recommendations for your
installation rather than copying values sized for another server.


Check the command-line configuration before configuring the web server:


C:\php\php.exe --ini
C:\php\php.exe -m



Set PHPRC to C:\php for the web-server and poller environments if needed to
select this configuration. An absolute path to php.exe avoids depending on
PATH for scheduled tasks. Ensure required DLLs are discoverable by both
identities; do not copy PHP DLLs into Windows system directories.


If loading external MIBs, set MIBDIRS to their actual directory and allow the
collecting account to update the Net-SNMP index/cache there if needed. This is
separate from permission to modify executable files.


After changing PHP configuration, recycle the application pool or restart the
selected web-server service. Confirm the Cacti installer reports the expected
configuration and modules for both web PHP and CLI PHP.


Configure the web server


IIS with FastCGI


Follow the PHP IIS instructions <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.iis.php> and Microsoft's
FastCGI configuration reference <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/iis/configuration/system.webserver/fastcgi/>.


* Enable the IIS CGI role service, which supplies FastCGI support.


* Create the Cacti site/application and a dedicated application pool. Match
the pool architecture to PHP. In Handler Mappings, map *.php through
FastCgiModule to C:\php\php-cgi.exe, not php.exe. Use the
corresponding
FastCGI application entry and allow script execution for the application.
PHP 8 does not provide php8isapi.dll; do not configure an ISAPI filter.


* Add index.php to the site's Default Document list. Apply the PHP CGI
settings required by the PHP IIS guide, including cgi.force_redirect=0
for IIS. Configure authentication/impersonation deliberately so the account
accessing Cacti files is known.


* Configure an HTTPS binding and restrict access during installation. Recycle
the affected application pool after configuration changes.




Apache


Follow the PHP Apache instructions <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.apache2.php> for a matching Apache/PHP
build. For Apache 2.4 with a compatible PHP 8 Thread-Safe module, the relevant
configuration is:


LoadModule php_module "C:/php/php8apache2_4.dll"
<FilesMatch "\.php$">
    SetHandler application/x-httpd-php
</FilesMatch>
DirectoryIndex index.php index.html
PHPIniDir "C:/php"



Check Apache's configuration before restarting its service. If using FastCGI
instead of the Apache module, follow that integration's PHP instructions and
use the appropriate PHP build.


File permissions


Identify the effective web identity and the scheduled-task account before
setting ACLs. For IIS, this may be IIS AppPool\Cacti for a pool named
Cacti, or another account selected by authentication and FastCGI
impersonation. See Microsoft's application-pool identity
guidance <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/iis/manage/configuring-security/application-pool-identities>. Do not assume the account is always IUSR.


Give the web and poller identities read access to Cacti's code and
configuration, and read/execute access to PHP, RRDtool, SNMP and optional Spine
programs and their dependencies. If PHP requires cmd.exe to launch a command,
allow only the necessary read/execute access. Do not grant either identity
write access to Windows system executables or to tool installation directories.


The poller needs Modify permission on rra and log and on the cache
subdirectories it uses. The web identity also needs Modify permission on log,
its configured temporary/session directories and the Cacti cache subdirectories
used by features such as real-time graphs. Use the installer's permission
checks to identify the required paths, including cache/boost,
cache/mibcache, cache/realtime and cache/spikekill. Grant access only to
the identities and paths that need it, accounting for inherited ACLs.


During installation or signed-package imports, Cacti also needs to write
queries and scripts under resource/snmp_queries, resource/script_server,
resource/script_queries and scripts. Grant temporary Modify access to the
identity performing that operation, then remove it when finished. The installer
also needs to initialize a nonempty CSRF secret, by default
include/vendor/csrf/csrf-secret.php. Allow creation in its containing
directory during setup, then retain read access to the generated secret and
remove the temporary directory write permission. Do not create an empty
placeholder secret.


Edit include/config.php as an administrator. Keep it readable by the web and
poller identities but not writable by them for ordinary operation. If a
specific installation operation needs to update it, grant temporary write
access for that operation and remove it afterward.


Create the database


The following commands are for Command Prompt, not PowerShell. Use the
actual path to mysql.exe or the equivalent MariaDB client if it is not on
PATH. The --password option prompts for the database administrator's
password; do not put that password on the command line.


mysql --user=root --password



Create an empty database and a dedicated application account. Replace
REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD with a unique secret before executing
this SQL. The example account and connection use TCP loopback consistently.


CREATE DATABASE cacti CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
CREATE USER 'cacti_user'@'127.0.0.1'
  IDENTIFIED BY 'REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD';
GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON cacti.* TO 'cacti_user'@'127.0.0.1';
GRANT SELECT ON mysql.time_zone_name TO 'cacti_user'@'127.0.0.1';



Exit the SQL client and import the schema from the same Cacti release you
extracted:


mysql --user=root --password --default-character-set=utf8mb4 cacti < "C:\inetpub\wwwroot\cacti\cacti.sql"



Ensure the server's time-zone tables are populated using its documented Windows
procedure. Granting access to an empty mysql.time_zone_name table does not
populate it. Resolve the database and time-zone checks reported by the Cacti
installer before completing installation.


Optional Cygwin and Spine configuration


If your Spine or RRDtool build depends on Cygwin, install the runtime packages
required by that build using the official
Cygwin installer <https://cygwin.com/>.
For a source build, follow the release's build instructions and select its
current compiler/development dependencies. Do not replace or delete DLLs
shipped with a binary distribution without checking that distribution's
requirements. Avoid mixing incompatible Cygwin runtimes.


For Spine, copy spine.conf.dist to spine.conf and set the connection
details to match Cacti. Replace the password placeholder with the same
application secret used above, not a sample password:


DB_Host     127.0.0.1
DB_Database cacti
DB_User     cacti_user
DB_Pass     REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD
DB_Port     3306



Restrict access to spine.conf to administrators and the poller account.
Verify Spine from that account before selecting it as the poller in Cacti.


Finish the Cacti installation


* Copy include/config.php.dist to include/config.php and set its database
connection. Replace the password placeholder with your application secret:


$database_default  = 'cacti';
$database_hostname = '127.0.0.1';
$database_username = 'cacti_user';
$database_password = 'REPLACE_WITH_UNIQUE_DATABASE_PASSWORD';
$database_port     = '3306';
$url_path          = '/cacti/';



Adjust $url_path if Cacti is served at another URL path.


* Open the Cacti URL through HTTPS. For a freshly imported development schema,
sign in with application username admin and initial password admin,
then choose a unique administrator password when prompted. These are
separate from the database credentials. Complete the installer and resolve
its dependency, database, path and permission checks before allowing
general access.


* In Console > Configuration > Settings > Paths, verify the executable
paths against your installation. For the example layout:





	Setting
	Example






	PHP binary
	C:/php/php.exe




	RRDtool
	C:/rrdtool/rrdtool.exe




	SNMP tools, if used
	C:/usr/bin/snmpget.exe, snmpwalk.exe, snmpbulkwalk.exe, snmpgetnext.exe




	Cacti log
	C:/inetpub/wwwroot/cacti/log/cacti.log




	Spine, if used
	C:/spine/spine.exe





Use the CLI PHP executable here, not the IIS FastCGI executable. Verify the
separate Spine configuration path if selecting Spine.


* Create devices and graphs deliberately, or configure discovery and
automation
rules for the intended network. Installing Cacti does not establish that
every discovered device exposes all measurements in its selected template.




Schedule and verify the poller


Log on as the intended poller account and test the same executable and paths
that the task will use:


C:\php\php.exe -q C:\inetpub\wwwroot\cacti\poller.php



Check log/cacti.log, data-source polling and graph updates. RRD files are
created for configured data sources; an installation without graphs need not
create them. Resolve permission and configuration errors before scheduling.


Open Task Scheduler and choose Create Task:


* Select the dedicated poller account and Run whether user is logged on or
not, with the account rights needed for batch logon. Do not enable
Run with highest privileges unless a documented local requirement needs
it.


* Add a daily trigger that repeats every 5 minutes indefinitely for the
default five-minute scheduler configuration. If changing Cacti's scheduler
interval, adjust this trigger to match it; the scheduler interval and data
source polling intervals are separate settings.


* Add a Start a program action with these separate fields:


* Program/script: C:\php\php.exe

* Add arguments: -q "C:\inetpub\wwwroot\cacti\poller.php"

* Start in: C:\inetpub\wwwroot\cacti



* Under Settings, choose Do not start a new instance if the task is
already running. Review any task execution limit against the configured
polling cycle; do not terminate a healthy poller prematurely.




Run the task manually, then verify subsequent scheduled runs, Task Scheduler's
result/history, Cacti's poller statistics and graph freshness. Recheck these
under the task account after changing PHP, paths, credentials or ACLs.


References


* Cacti development (1.3) PHP dependency checks <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/lib/utility.php>

* Cacti development (1.3) installation permission checks <https://github.com/Cacti/cacti/blob/develop/lib/installer.php>

* PHP Windows installation <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.manual.php>, IIS <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.iis.php> and
Apache <https://www.php.net/manual/en/install.windows.apache2.php>

* Microsoft FastCGI configuration <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/iis/configuration/system.webserver/fastcgi/> and
application-pool identities <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/iis/manage/configuring-security/application-pool-identities>

* MySQL 8 removed features <https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.0/en/upgrading-from-previous-series.html>

* Microsoft Task Scheduler <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/taskschd/task-scheduler-start-page>
